[external_agent_tool_result]
## Operating Context

The platform sits inside a coaching practice that runs partly outside it. Future work must not assume the app is the whole system.

- Coaching sessions happen on video and are transcribed. Session records, notes, and recaps originate outside the app.
- Booking and payment run through their own flow (packages, booking passes, a public booking microsite, single sessions).
- Students also practice on third-party platforms. Work done elsewhere is real work, and the app is not the only record of a student's effort.
- Joshua authors material ahead of time and assigns it, rather than the system generating it on demand.
- Students use both desktop and mobile, in focused study blocks and in short check-ins.

## Capabilities and Constraints

Confirmed surfaces: authentication and onboarding, the foyer, the classroom, guided drills and the question bank, full-passage Reading Comprehension, the Activity Centre, the Testing Centre and practice test runs, the wrong answer journal, saved sets, analytics and history, scheduling and booking, the inbox, the bootcamps, and an admin and teacher workspace.

Technical constraints:

- React, TypeScript, Vite, Tailwind, shadcn/ui and Radix primitives, React Router, TanStack Query, Supabase.
- One motion library (`framer-motion`). One primary icon library (`lucide-react`). New work composes existing primitives before introducing a new dependency.
- Deployment is not a side effect of pushing to `main`. Production at lsatprep.study requires an explicit publish step.
- Database migrations in `supabase/migrations/` are an archive, not an applied ledger. Production schema changes go through their own path, and UI must not assume a table exists because a migration file does.

Terminology is not defined here. It is defined per bounded context and mapped by `CONTEXT-MAP.md`. A word can carry different weight in different contexts, and collapsing them has already caused real bugs (a **flag** is not a **wrong answer**; **Set** and **Position** were separated for this reason).

## Brand Commitments

- **Name:** LSAT U, operating under Aspiring Attorneys. Production domain lsatprep.study.
- **No em-dashes in anything a student or prospect reads.** Joshua does not type them. A stray em-dash reads as machine-written and costs trust. This binds computed copy, utility copy, and authored copy alike.
- **Voice: undecided, deliberately.** The previous record carried a "coach voice, not industry voice" principle and two named voice registers (R1 procedural, R2 metaphor-led). Both were removed on 2026-09-05 by Joshua's decision, on the grounds that they were not stated clearly enough to be usable. They are being redone. Until a replacement is written, no work should cite the old voice rules, and no work should invent a voice rule to fill the gap. Route student-facing copy to Joshua.

## Evidence on Hand

Real:

- A live production deployment at lsatprep.study with real enrolled students.
- Authored LSAT curriculum: bootcamp modules, guided drill banks, RC passages with per-question explanations, and practice test content.
- Real student work in the database: attempts, sessions, flags, wrong answers, assignments.
- A real named instructor with a real coaching practice behind the product.

Absent, and never to be fabricated:

- No testimonials, case studies, press coverage, score-improvement statistics, or named customer references exist in this repo. Do not write them into any surface.
- No pricing, licensing, or availability claims are established here.
- No third-party or copyright framing anywhere in student-facing copy.

## Product Principles

1. **Truthfulness over performance.** Every number, badge, streak, status, and progress claim traces to real stored data. An honest empty state beats invented content. If nothing has been assigned, the product says so and stops. This is the one principle carried forward unchanged from the previous record, and it is the hardest of the five.

2. **Compute t...

## assistant

[external_agent_tool_result]
Run systematic **technical** quality checks and generate a comprehensive report. Don't fix issues — document them for other commands to address.

This is a code-level audit, not a design critique. Check what's measurable and verifiable in the implementation.

## Diagnostic Scan

Run comprehensive checks across 5 dimensions. Score each dimension 0-4 using the criteria below.

### 1. Accessibility (A11y)

**Check for**:
- **Contrast issues**: Text contrast ratios < 4.5:1 (or 7:1 for AAA)
- **Missing ARIA**: Interactive elements without proper roles, labels, or states
- **Keyboard navigation**: Missing focus indicators, illogical tab order, keyboard traps
- **Semantic HTML**: Improper heading hierarchy, missing landmarks, divs instead of buttons
- **Alt text**: Missing or poor image descriptions
- **Form issues**: Inputs without labels, poor error messaging, missing required indicators

**Score 0-4**: 0=Inaccessible (fails WCAG A), 1=Major gaps (few ARIA labels, no keyboard nav), 2=Partial (some a11y effort, significant gaps), 3=Good (WCAG AA mostly met, minor gaps), 4=Excellent (WCAG AA fully met, approaches AAA)

### 2. Performance

**Check for**:
- **Layout thrashing**: Reading/writing layout properties in loops
- **Expensive animations**: Casual layout-property animation, unbounded blur/filter/shadow effects, or effects that visibly drop frames
- **Missing optimization**: Images without lazy loading, unoptimized assets, missing will-change
- **Bundle size**: Unnecessary imports, unused dependencies
- **Render performance**: Unnecessary re-renders, missing memoization

**Score 0-4**: 0=Severe issues (layout thrash, unoptimized everything), 1=Major problems (no lazy loading, expensive animations), 2=Partial (some optimization, gaps remain), 3=Good (mostly optimized, minor improvements possible), 4=Excellent (fast, lean, well-optimized)

### 3. Theming

**Check for**:
- **Hard-coded colors**: Colors not using design tokens
- **Broken dark mode**: Missing dark mode variants, poor contrast in dark theme
- **Inconsistent tokens**: Using wrong tokens, mixing token types
- **Theme switching issues**: Values that don't update on theme change

**Score 0-4**: 0=No theming (hard-coded everything), 1=Minimal tokens (mostly hard-coded), 2=Partial (tokens exist but inconsistently used), 3=Good (tokens used, minor hard-coded values), 4=Excellent (full token system, dark mode works perfectly)

### 4. Responsive Design

**Check for**:
- **Fixed widths**: Hard-coded widths that break on mobile
- **Touch targets**: Interactive elements < 44x44px
- **Horizontal scroll**: Content overflow on narrow viewports
- **Text scaling**: Layouts that break when text size increases
- **Missing breakpoints**: No mobile/tablet variants

**Score 0-4**: 0=Desktop-only (breaks on mobile), 1=Major issues (some breakpoints, many failures), 2=Partial (works on mobile, rough edges), 3=Good (responsive, minor touch target or overflow issues), 4=Excellent (fluid, all viewports, proper touch targets)

### 5. Anti-Patterns (CRITICAL)

Check against ALL the **DON'T** guidelines from the parent impeccable skill (already loaded in this context). Look for AI slop tells (AI color palette, gradient text, glassmorphism, hero metrics, card grids, generic fonts) and general design anti-patterns (gray on color, nested cards, bounce easing, redundant copy).

**Score 0-4**: 0=AI slop gallery (5+ tells), 1=Heavy AI aesthetic (3-4 tells), 2=Some tells (1-2 noticeable), 3=Mostly clean (subtle issues only), 4=No AI tells (distinctive, intentional design)

## Generate Report

### Audit Health Score

| # | Dimension | Score | Key Finding |
|---|-----------|-------|-------------|
| 1 | Accessibility | ? | [most critical a11y issue or "--"] |
| 2 | Performance | ? | |
| 3 | Responsive Design | ? | |
| 4 | Theming | ? | |
| 5 | Anti-Patterns | ? | |
| **Total** | | **??/20** | **[Rating band]** |

    11	
    12	/**
    13	 * The section list, repeated inside the stage for narrow windows.
    14	 *
    15	 * The third column that normally holds this list is hidden below 1280px, and
    16	 * below 1400px for Wrong answers, where it collapses behind a trigger that
    17	 * reads as nothing more than a section title. At those widths every index
    18	 * route was telling the student to "choose an entry from the list" while no
    19	 * list was anywhere on screen — which covers a 13" laptop and any window with
    20	 * a panel docked.
    21	 *
    22	 * Both copies are always rendered and CSS shows exactly one, so there is no
    23	 * width measured in JS and nothing to re-run on resize. `display: none` also
    24	 * keeps the hidden copy out of the accessibility tree, so it is not read twice.
    25	 */
    26	export function StageIndexList({
    27	  items,
    28	  label,
    29	  heading,
    30	}: {
    31	  items: StageIndexItem[];
    32	  label: string;
    33	  /** Optional group label, shown only when a page renders more than one list. */
    34	  heading?: string;
    35	}) {
    36	  const navigate = useNavigate();
    37	  if (items.length === 0) return null;
    38	
    39	  return (
    40	    <nav className="classroom-stage-index" aria-label={label}>
    41	      {heading ? (
    42	        <p className="classroom-stage-index-heading">{heading}</p>
    43	      ) : null}
    44	      {items.map((item) => (
    45	        <button
    46	          type="button"
    47	          key={item.key}
    48	          className="classroom-panel-item"
    49	          onClick={() => navigate(item.route)}
    50	        >
    51	          <h3>
    52	            {item.unread ? (
    53	              <span className="classroom-unread-dot" aria-hidden="true" />
    54	            ) : null}
    55	            {item.title}
    56	            {item.unread ? <span className="sr-only"> (unread)</span> : null}
    57	          </h3>
    58	          <time>{item.meta}</time>
    59	        </button>
    60	      ))}
    61	    </nav>
    62	  );
    63	}
    64

.classroom-root .classroom-section-heading button {
  align-items: center;
  background: transparent;
  border: 0;
  cursor: pointer;
  display: flex;
  justify-content: center;
  margin-left: auto;
  padding: 4px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-panel-list {
  display: grid;
  gap: 4px;
  margin: 0 -8px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-panel-item {
  background: transparent;
  border: 0;
  border-radius: var(--classroom-radius);
  cursor: pointer;
  padding: 8px;
  text-align: left;
  transition: background-color var(--classroom-transition) ease;
  width: 100%;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-panel-item:hover {
  background: var(--classroom-hover);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-panel-item-active {
  background: var(--classroom-active);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-panel-item h3 {
  color: var(--classroom-ink);
  font-size: 14px;
  font-weight: 500;
  line-height: 1.45;
  margin: 0 0 4px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-panel-item time {
  color: var(--classroom-meta-soft);
  font-size: 0.75rem;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-panel-item-active h3,
.classroom-root .classroom-panel-item-active time {
  color: var(--classroom-active-ink);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-panel-item-active time {
  opacity: 0.6;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-list-state {
  display: grid;
  gap: 10px;
  padding: 4px 0;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-list-state span,
.classroom-root .classroom-skeleton {
  background: var(--classroom-line);
  border-radius: 4px;
  display: block;
  height: 13px;
  opacity: 0.75;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-list-state span:nth-child(2) {
  width: 72%;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-list-state span:nth-child(3) {
  width: 84%;
  border-radius: var(--classroom-radius);
  cursor: pointer;
  display: none;
  font-size: 14px;
  height: 48px;
  justify-content: space-between;
  margin-bottom: 32px;
  padding: 0 16px;
  width: 100%;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-dateline {
  align-items: center;
  color: var(--classroom-meta-soft);
  display: flex;
  font-size: 14px;
  font-weight: 500;
  gap: 6px;
  margin-bottom: 16px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-title {
  font-size: 30px;
  font-weight: 700;
  letter-spacing: -0.02em;
  line-height: 1.15;
  margin: 0 0 24px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-message {
  border: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
  border-radius: var(--classroom-radius);
  margin-bottom: 48px;
  padding: 20px;
  transition: border-color var(--classroom-transition) ease;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-message header {
  align-items: center;
  display: flex;
  gap: 10px;
  display: flex;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  gap: 8px;
  margin-top: 16px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-button {
  background: transparent;
  border: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
  border-radius: var(--classroom-radius);
  cursor: pointer;
  font-family: "Outfit", ui-sans-serif, sans-serif;
  font-size: 13px;
  font-weight: 500;
  padding: 8px 14px;
  transition:
    background-color var(--classroom-transition) ease,
    border-color var(--classroom-transition) ease;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-button:hover {
  background: var(--classroom-hover);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-button-primary {
  background: var(--classroom-link);
  border-color: var(--classroom-link);
  color: #ffffff;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-button-primary:hover {
  background: var(--classroom-link);
}

.classroom-root .classroom-content-section {
  margin-bottom: 48px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-section-header {
  align-items: baseline;
  display: flex;
  position: absolute;
  width: 100%;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-inline-error {
  align-items: center;
  border: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
  border-radius: var(--classroom-radius);
  display: flex;
  gap: 12px;
  padding: 12px 14px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-inline-error p {
  font-size: 14px;
  margin: 0;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-inline-error .classroom-button {
  margin-left: auto;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-skeleton-stack {
  display: grid;
  gap: 10px;
}

function JournalSectionList({
  activeId,
  onClose,
}: {
  activeId: string | undefined;
  onClose?: () => void;
}) {
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const query = useJournalEntries();
  const [filter, setFilter] = React.useState("");
  const rows = React.useMemo(() => {
    const source = query.data ?? [];
    const needle = filter.trim().toLowerCase();
    if (!needle) return source;
    return source.filter(
      (row) =>
        row.label.toLowerCase().includes(needle) ||
        row.qid.toLowerCase().includes(needle),
    );
  }, [filter, query.data]);

  const openRow = (qid: string) => {
    onClose?.();
    navigate(`/journal/${encodeURIComponent(qid)}`);
  };

  return (
          type="button"
          className="classroom-mobile-menu-button"
          onClick={() => setMobileMenuOpen(true)}
          aria-label="Open classroom menu"
        >
          <Menu size={19} />
        </button>

        <aside className="classroom-sidebar">
          <ClassroomSidebar
            displayName={sidebarName}
            activeSection={activeSection}
            onSection={openSection}
            onHome={goHome}
          />
        </aside>

        {mobileMenuOpen ? (
          <div className="classroom-mobile-menu">
            <button
              type="button"
              className="classroom-overlay-backdrop"
              onClick={() => setMobileMenuOpen(false)}
              aria-label="Close classroom menu"
            />
            <aside>
              <button
                type="button"
                className="classroom-overlay-close"
                onClick={() => setMobileMenuOpen(false)}
                aria-label="Close classroom menu"
              >
                <X size={18} />
              </button>
              <ClassroomSidebar
                displayName={sidebarName}
                activeSection={activeSection}
                onSection={(section) => {
                  setMobileMenuOpen(false);
                  openSection(section);
                }}
                onHome={goHome}
                onNavigate={() => setMobileMenuOpen(false)}
              />
            </aside>
          </div>
        ) : null}

        {activeSection ? (
          <aside className="classroom-section-list">
            <SectionList section={activeSection} activeId={activeId} />
          </aside>
        ) : null}

        {journalDetail ? (
          <React.Suspense fallback={<StageFallback />}>
            <Outlet context={contextValue} />
          </React.Suspense>
        ) : messagesStage ? (
          <main className="classroom-messages-stage">
            <React.Suspense fallback={<StageFallback />}>
              <Outlet context={contextValue} />
            </React.Suspense>
          </main>
        ) : workspaceStage ? (
          <main className="classroom-workspace-stage">
            <React.Suspense fallback={<StageFallback />}>
              <Outlet context={contextValue} />
            </React.Suspense>
          </main>
        ) : (

                  type="button"

  private async mergeExplanationSidecars(): Promise<void> {
    const pts = new Set<number>();
    for (const q of this.questions.values()) pts.add(q.pt);
    const results = await Promise.all(
      Array.from(pts).map(async (pt) => {
        try {
          const res = await fetch(`/data/explanations/PT${pt}-explanations.json`);
          if (!res.ok) return null;
          const json = await res.json();
          return json && typeof json === 'object' ? (json as Record<string, LRExplanation>) : null;
        } catch {
          return null;
        }
      }),
    );
    let attached = 0;
    for (const sidecar of results) {
      if (!sidecar) continue;
      for (const [qid, expl] of Object.entries(sidecar)) {
        const q = this.questions.get(qid);
        if (!q) continue;
        q.explanations = expl;
        if (!q.answerChoiceExplanations) {
          q.answerChoiceExplanations = this.deriveTypedExplanations(expl, q.correctAnswer);
        }
        attached += 1;
      }
    }
    if (attached > 0) {
      console.log(`Question bank: attached LSAT Lab explanations to ${attached} question(s).`);
    }
  }

  private deriveTypedExplanations(
    expl: LRExplanation,
    correctAnswer: LRQuestion['correctAnswer'],
  ): LRQuestion['answerChoiceExplanations'] | undefined {
    const style = expl.styles?.standard ?? expl.styles?.brief ?? expl.styles?.anotherWay;
    if (!style) return undefined;
    const out: any = {};
    for (const L of ['A', 'B', 'C', 'D', 'E'] as const) {
      const c = style.choices?.[L];
      const isCorrect = L === (expl.correctChoice ?? correctAnswer);

function routeNeedsQuestionBank(pathname: string): boolean {
  return !BANK_FREE_PREFIXES.some(
    (prefix) => pathname === prefix || pathname.startsWith(`${prefix}/`),
  );
}

export function QuestionBankProvider({ children }: { children: ReactNode }) {
  const { pathname } = useLocation();

  const [manifest, setManifest] = useState<QuestionManifest | null>(() =>
    questionBank.isReady() ? questionBank.getManifest() : null,
  );
  // Must start true on a route that will load, or the first render hands consumers
  // isLoading=false with an empty bank and they flash "not found" before the fetch
  // lands. Consumers gate their direct questionBank.getQuestion() calls on this.
  const [isLoading, setIsLoading] = useState<boolean>(
    () => !questionBank.isReady() && routeNeedsQuestionBank(pathname),
  );
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (questionBank.isReady()) {
      setManifest(questionBank.getManifest());
      setIsLoading(false);
      return;
    }

    // Re-evaluated on every navigation, so entering a consumer route from a
    // bank-free one still loads. questionBank.load() dedupes concurrent callers
    // and returns immediately once ready, so repeat calls are free.
    if (!routeNeedsQuestionBank(pathname)) {
      setIsLoading(false);
      return;
    }

    let cancelled = false;
    setIsLoading(true);

    questionBank
      .load()
      .then(() => {
        if (cancelled) return;
        setManifest(questionBank.getManifest());
        setIsLoading(false);
      })
      .catch((err) => {
        if (cancelled) return;
        console.error('Failed to load question bank:', err);
        setError('Failed to load questions');
        setIsLoading(false);
      });

    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
    };
  }, [pathname]);

  return (
    <QuestionBankContext.Provider value={{ manifest, isLoading, error }}>
      {children}
    </QuestionBankContext.Provider>

import { createContext, useContext, useEffect, useState, type ReactNode } from "react";
import { useLocation } from "react-router-dom";
import { questionBank, QuestionManifest } from '@/lib/questionLoader';

interface QuestionBankContextType {
  manifest: QuestionManifest | null;
  isLoading: boolean;
  error: string | null;
}

const QuestionBankContext = createContext<QuestionBankContextType | undefined>(undefined);

/**
 * Routes that provably never read the question bank, verified by grep: nothing
 * under src/features/activity-centre/, src/pages/Auth.tsx, or src/features/rc/
 * imports questionLoader or useQuestionBank. Every /rc route — player, results,
 * search, redirect — renders out of src/features/rc/ and reads the RC bank in
 * public/data/rc/ instead, so a student opening one passage was downloading the
 * whole Logical Reasoning bank and firing an explanation request per PrepTest
 * before the passage could paint.
 *
 * This is a DENYLIST on purpose. The bank is ~5.5MB raw (1.4MB gzipped) and costs
 * ~111ms of blocked main thread plus 9-14 explanation fetches, and the provider sits
 * on the catch-all route (App.tsx), so every page paid for it — including /auth,
 * before the student had even signed in.
 *
 * An allowlist would be the tidier shape and is the eventual fix (a provider per
 * consumer route group). It is not safe to write yet: 24 modules read the
 * `questionBank` singleton directly without subscribing to this context, so
 * "does this route call useQuestionBank()" is not a reliable test for "does this
 * route need the bank" — /analytics is the clearest example. Getting an allowlist
 * wrong renders a page with silently empty questions; getting this denylist wrong
 * is impossible for any route not named in it, because every other route keeps
 * exactly the behaviour it had before.
 *
 * Add to this list only after grepping the route's whole subtree for questionLoader.
 */
const BANK_FREE_PREFIXES = ['/activities', '/activity-lab', '/auth', '/rc'] as const;

function routeNeedsQuestionBank(pathname: string): boolean {

> **Additional context needed**: what the interface is trying to accomplish.

### Gather Assessments

Launch two independent assessments. **Neither may see the other's output** — this isolation is what makes the combined score honest. Running both in one head silently anchors them to each other; do not shortcut it for cost, speed, or context-size reasons.

Delegate each assessment to a separate sub-agent (Claude Code's `Agent` tool, Codex's subagent spawning, etc.). Each returns structured findings as text. Do NOT output findings to the user yet.

Fall back to sequential in-head work only if the environment genuinely cannot spawn sub-agents.

**Tab isolation**: When browser automation is available, each assessment MUST create its own new tab. Never reuse an existing tab, even if one is already open at the correct URL. This prevents the two assessments from interfering with each other's page state.

#### Assessment A: LLM Design Review

Read the relevant source files (HTML, CSS, JS/TS) and, if browser automation is available, visually inspect the live page. **Create a new tab** for this; do not reuse existing tabs. After navigation, label the tab by setting the document title:
```javascript
document.title = '[LLM] ' + document.title;
```
Think like a design director. Evaluate:

**AI Slop Detection (CRITICAL)**: Does this look like every other AI-generated interface? Review against ALL **DON'T** guidelines from the parent impeccable skill (already loaded in this context). Check for AI color palette, gradient text, dark glows, glassmorphism, hero metric layouts, identical card grids, generic fonts, and all other tells. **The test**: If someone said "AI made this," would you believe them immediately?

**Holistic Design Review**: visual hierarchy (eye flow, primary action clarity), information architecture (structure, grouping, cognitive load), emotional resonance (does it match brand and audience?), discoverability (are interactive elements obvious?), composition (balance, whitespace, rhythm), typography (hierarchy, readability, font choices), color (purposeful use, cohesion, accessibility), states & edge cases (empty, loading, error, success), microcopy (clarity, tone, helpfulness).

**Cognitive Load** (consult [cognitive-load](cognitive-load.md)):
- Run the 8-item cognitive load checklist. Report failure count: 0-1 = low (good), 2-3 = moderate, 4+ = critical.
- Count visible options at each decision point. If >4, flag it.
- Check for progressive disclosure: is complexity revealed only when needed?

**Emotional Journey**:
- What emotion does this interface evoke? Is that intentional?
- **Peak-end rule**: Is the most intense moment positive? Does the experience end well?
- **Emotional valleys**: Check for anxiety spikes at high-stakes moments (payment, delete, commit). Are there design interventions (progress indicators, reassurance copy, undo options)?

**Nielsen's Heuristics** (consult [heuristics-scoring](heuristics-scoring.md)):
Score each of the 10 heuristics 0-4. This scoring will be presented in the report.

Return structured findings covering: AI slop verdict, heuristic scores, cognitive load assessment, what's working (2-3 items), priority issues (3-5 with what/why/fix), minor observations, and provocative questions.

#### Assessment B: Automated Detection

Run the bundled deterministic detector, which flags 25 specific patterns (AI slop tells + general design quality).

**CLI scan**:
```bash
npx impeccable --json [--fast] [target]
```

- Pass HTML/JSX/TSX/Vue/Svelte files or directories as `[target]` (anything with markup). Do not pass CSS-only files.
- For URLs, skip the CLI scan (it requires Puppeteer). Use browser visualization instead.
- For large directories (200+ scannable files), use `--fast` (regex-only, skips jsdom)
- For 500+ files, narrow scope or ask the user
- Exit code 0 = clean, 2 = findings

**Browser visualization** — **required** when browser auto...

# `$impeccable audit` — Notes and recaps

**11/20 — Acceptable, significant work needed.** Measured on live production, signed in, light theme only.

| Dimension | Score | Finding |
|---|---|---|
| Accessibility | 2/4 | Every date on the page renders at **2.10:1** |
| Performance | 1/4 | **1.79 MB** of question explanations on a 3-row text page |
| Responsive | 3/4 | Column swap is correct; main column is empty at ≥1280 |
| Theming | 2/4 | Two undefined tokens; documented body font never renders |
| Anti-patterns | 3/4 | No AI slop — genuinely restrained |

### The three that matter

**Every date fails AA at 2.10:1.** `--classroom-meta-soft` is `rgba(67,64,60,0.4)` in light — an alpha, not a resolved colour. It breaks the row dates, the count hint, the dateline and note descriptions in one stroke. It matters concretely: two of your three live notes are "Conditional Logic: the Cheat Sheet" and "…(five-section cut)", and the date is the only thing separating them. The fix already exists — `--classroom-meta-text: #6d6a67` is defined one line away and unused at these sites.

**1.79 MB transferred / 7.18 MB decoded, 44% of the page, on three rows of text.** `/classroom/recaps` isn't in `BANK_FREE_PREFIXES`, so it loads the whole LR bank plus one explanation fetch per PrepTest. I measured **59 requests, 33 of them 404s**. The comment on that denylist documents "9-14 explanation fetches" — it's stale by 4-6x. The bank grew and nobody re-measured.

**A student with no notes sees the title and nothing else.** `ClassroomRouteViews.tsx:215` is a ternary with `: null` and no else branch. The rail is the same — `classroom-panel-empty` is gated on flagged and journal only. Against PRODUCT.md principle 1, and it lands hardest on a brand-new client whose first recap isn't written yet. Your own copy for it already exists in `RecapsSection.tsx:20`.

### Two corrections to things I said mid-run

The dark theme is **fine** (8.27:1 / 19.16:1) — my first dark screenshot caught a mid-transition frame. And the active row is fine at 10.31:1; I misread a small screenshot. Neither is a finding. Nothing persisted from that test: it was a CSS class flip in my tab, reverted, never your code or your saved preference.

The detector's one "side-stripe border" hit is also a **false positive** — it's a `blockquote` rule, which is the legitimate use.

### What's working

The focus ring is genuinely good — verified with a real keyboard Tab, 2px ink outline, no fade-in; the `:where()` floor from 2026-09-05 is doing exactly its job. The 1280px column swap is correct at all three widths, and I checked specifically for the double-read the `StageIndexList` comment claims to prevent — there isn't one. Unread state survives the broken accent token because it never relied on colour.

Full detail in [NOTES-RECAPS-AUDIT.md](projects/lsatu/NOTES-RECAPS-AUDIT.md), including four P2s (overlay isn't a dialog, redundant "Open the list", undefined tokens, Lexend never rendering) and three P3s.

The critique's second assessment is still running — I'll fold it in with the scored heuristics when it lands.

---

# Assessment A — LLM Design Review: "Notes and recaps" (`/classroom/recaps`)

**Method:** read `PRODUCT.md`, `DESIGN.md`, `src/components/classroom/CONTEXT.md`, and the five named source files; inspected the live production page in my own tab (tab-2) in the shipped light theme at 1440×900 and 375×812, with measured computed styles and contrast ratios.

**Caveat on the data I saw:** I was signed in as Joshua (`is_admin`, role label "Teacher"), so the rail carried an extra "Teaching" group a student never sees, and the three notes present are his own test content — one is literally titled *"Verification: unread notes"* with the body *"Safe to delete."* That is not a design finding, but it does mean I could not observe a real student's note mix. Everything below about the zero-note path comes from reading the code, not from mutating data.

---

## 1. AI slop verdict

**No. This does not read as AI-made, and it is not close.** It reads as hand-built and opinionated — a warm paper room with hairline separation and nothing else. Against the absolute bans:

| Ban | Status |
|---|---|
| Side-stripe borders (>1px coloured leading edge) | **Clean** on this surface. The only >1px left borders in `classroom.css` are a blockquote rule (`classroom.css:2343`) and journal callouts (`:2704`, `:2749`), none of which render here. |
| Gradient text | **Clean.** No `background-clip: text` anywhere. |
| Decorative glassmorphism | **Clean** here. (`--observatory-glass*` exists at `classroom.css:876-880` but is scoped to `.classroom-root-drill`.) |
| Hero-metric template | **Clean.** There is no big number. The one metric — "All 3 read." — is a 15px sentence. |
| Identical card grids | **Clean.** Rows, not cards. No grid. |
| Modal-as-first-thought | **Mostly clean** — the drawer is a responsive fallback, not the primary. But see P1-4: below 1280px it fires *alongside* the inline list, which is modal-as-second-thought. |

Against the project's own DESIGN.md bans, three real hits:

- **A second typeface in Workroom chrome — confirmed.** `classroom.css:39` sets `.classroom-root { font-family: "Outfit", … }`, and `<body>` is Lexend. Measured: `h1.classroom-title` renders `Outfit` 700/36px; `body` renders `Lexend`. DESIGN.md's One Face Rule allows Outfit only as the *question lock* (LSAT stimulus and answer choices). This is the whole classroom shell, including the rail. DESIGN.md's sanctioned scoped-world list (`DESIGN.md:174`) names `.mc-bootcamp`, `.ff-bootcamp`, `.rc-bootcamp`, `.topic-abstract`, `.cs-punk` and the eight Activity worlds — `.classroom-root` is not on it, and DESIGN.md:156 says "Rooms may be worlds; corridors never are." The classroom rail *is* the corridor. Either DESIGN.md is stale here or the classroom is out of bounds; the record as written says out of bounds.
- **Colour used for something other than meaning — confirmed.** `--classroom-link: #ff8906` (`classroom.css:14`) fills the Messages unread badge; the panel-wash gradients (`classroom.css:130-146`) paint a decorative warm sheen on the rail and list card. Neither says correct, incorrect, warn, or danger.
- **A token that resolves to nothing — confirmed, and it is the exact failure DESIGN.md's Named Ground Rule was written for.** `.classroom-unread-dot` and `.classroom-unread-count` consume `var(--classroom-accent, …)` and `var(--classroom-surface, #fff)` at `classroom.css:4514`, `:4528-4529`. Neither variable is defined anywhere in `src/`. The unread dot therefore falls back to `currentColor` — the same ink as the note title next to it — so the "unread" marker renders as a bullet point. Meanwhile the *Messages* unread badge is orange. Two unread counters in one sidebar, two different co...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

## assistant

# `$impeccable critique` — 21/40, and the plan

Both assessments ran in isolation and never saw each other. They converged on seven findings independently — that agreement is the strongest signal in the report.

**Anti-patterns: pass.** Both concluded this does not look AI-generated. The detector returned zero findings on the two view files, and I measured **zero box-shadowed elements** under `.classroom-root`. The one "side-stripe" hit is a `blockquote` — false positive. As the design review put it: the failure mode here is the opposite of slop. Slop is over-decorated sameness; this is under-designed emptiness.

**Where it scores worst:** Flexibility 1/4 (no search, filter or grouping — Flagged and Journal both got one, Notes didn't, and at 25 sessions/week this list is in the hundreds within a year). Then five 2s.

### What the second assessment caught that I missed

I'd only measured the stage. It measured the whole shell and found more AA failures, which I then verified myself:

- Rail group labels (CLASSROOM/STUDY/REVIEW) — **2.54:1**
- Role label "Student"/"Teacher" — **2.54:1**
- Messages unread badge, white on `#ff8906` — **2.38:1**
- Rail rows are **34px** tall against PRODUCT.md's 44px floor — and the same class draws the mobile drawer, so that's a real touch failure
- `.classroom-panel-search:focus { outline: none }` — the exact anti-pattern DESIGN.md names by hand

It also found the fix I'd under-estimated: `--classroom-meta-text: #6d6a67` measures **5.37:1**, and `classroom.css:15-22` already contains a comment arguing for exactly this swap. The tokens exist and are used twice against 15 uses of the broken one. **Phase 1 is a token swap, not a redesign.**

### The plan

22 findings, 9 phases. Phases 1–6 close 11 of them including all three P0 contrast items, need no decisions from you, and are mostly token swaps and prop threading — that's roughly the first day.

Two things I did *not* decide for you, because they change the work materially:

**A — what is `/classroom/recaps` for?** It renders 89px of content into a 900px column. Either make it a real landing (newest unread note inline + one line of orientation), or admit it's a redirect and open the newest note like a mail client. I recommend the first; Principle 2 is explicit and this is where "a real coach is in it" is weakest.

**B — is the classroom a world or a corridor?** `classroom.css:39` overrides the body face, so Lexend renders nowhere in the classroom despite being downloaded, and Roboto Mono stands in for the documented IBM Plex Mono. Either sanction it in DESIGN.md and swap the mono, or delete line 39 and come back to Lexend. I recommend sanctioning it — both assessments called the warm Outfit aesthetic a genuine asset. The defect is that it's undocumented, not that it exists.

Full detail in [NOTES-RECAPS-PLAN.md](projects/lsatu/NOTES-RECAPS-PLAN.md).

One thing worth flagging before any of this ships: per PRODUCT.md, pushing to `main` doesn't deploy — lsatprep.study needs the explicit publish step. Every phase in the plan ends at "merged", not "live".

const fs=require('fs');const lines=fs.readFileSync('src/components/classroom/classroom.css','utf8').split(/\r?\n/);
function selFor(i){for(let j=i;j>=0;j--){if(lines[j].includes('{')){let s=[];for(let k=j;k>=0&&k>j-6;k--){const t=lines[k].trim();if(t.endsWith(',')||t.includes('{'))s.unshift(t.replace('{','').trim());else break;}return s.join(' ');}}return '?';}
lines.forEach((l,i)=>{
  const m=l.match(/^\s*opacity:\s*(0?\.\d+|0)\s*;/);
  if(!m) return;
  const sel=selFor(i);
  if(/nav|profile|panel-item|dateline|empty-copy|meta|badge|group/.test(sel)) console.log((i+1)+' | opacity:'+m[1]+' | '+sel.slice(0,95));
});

.classroom-root .classroom-nav-badge {
  align-items: center;
  background: var(--classroom-badge-fill);
  border-radius: 999px;
  color: var(--classroom-badge-ink);
  display: inline-flex;
  flex: 0 0 auto;

## assistant

Phase 1 is implemented and verified. Let me show you where it stands.

## Phase 1 complete — contrast

**Changed** in `classroom.css`:
- **64 text sites** swapped from the fill tokens (`--classroom-meta-soft`, `rgb(var(--classroom-meta))`) to `--classroom-meta-text`. The 4 border/fill uses were left alone — that's the split the file's own comment already asked for.
- **New `--classroom-badge-fill` / `--classroom-badge-ink` pair.** I couldn't just reuse `--classroom-link-text`: it's `#a25704` in light but a bright `#ff8906` in dark, so reusing it would have traded a light failure for a dark one. Each theme now picks the pairing its own ground allows — 5.38:1 light, 8.06:1 dark.
- **Removed 5 `opacity` declarations** on muted text. This was the part neither assessment caught: the group labels were already going to be `#6d6a67`, but a `0.70` opacity multiplied on top and held them at 2.92:1. A token swap alone would not have fixed the sidebar.

**Verified** on the live app with those exact rules applied:

| Route | Nodes checked | Failures | Minimum |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/classroom/recaps` | 44 | **0** | **4.90:1** |
| `/classroom/item/:id` (chrome) | 39 | **0** | **4.90:1** |

CSS parses clean at 646 rules. In the screenshot the dates, the group labels and the badge are all legible where they were washed out before.

Two things you should know rather than take on trust:

**The verification was a faithful DOM check, not a build.** I applied the exact rule changes to the live page and measured every text node. I could not serve my own build — port 8080 is held by another chat's dev server, and I won't kill someone else's. Nothing I did persisted; it was a CSS injection in my browser tab only.

Along the way I caught myself twice: my first probe used `!important` too broadly and reported the active-nav chevron at 1.49:1. That was my measurement breaking the cascade, not the file — `.classroom-nav-active em` already carries the right colour and wins on specificity. Corrected before I drew any conclusion from it.

### A new finding, and it's a real one

On the note detail I split chrome from authored content:

- Chrome: 39 nodes, **0 failures**
- The note's own content: 564 nodes, **20 failures, worst 2.03:1**

The "Conditional Logic: the Cheat Sheet" note carries inline styles with text at 2.03:1 — "sufficient", "necessary", "left", "right". That's your teaching content, not `classroom.css`, so no token change reaches it. It's ledger item #17 with a price tag attached, and it's arguably P1 on its own since it's student-facing LSAT material below the AA gate.

I have not committed. Nine files including `classroom.css` already had your uncommitted changes in them, and I won't sweep those into a commit of mine.

## assistant

Two corrections you need before the next step, both mine.

## I was wrong about your note content

I told you the Cheat Sheet note had **20 elements at 2.03:1** and called it arguably P1. That was a bug in my measuring probe, not a problem in your content. I was treating any background with alpha > 0 as opaque, so a `rgba(0,0,0,0.04)` tint over white got scored as solid black.

Re-measured with the ancestor background stack composited properly: **one** failure in 564 authored text nodes — a 10px uppercase label ("the trigger") at **3.90:1** on a grey chip.

Your note also authors against the design tokens (`var(--classroom-meta-text)`, `var(--classroom-link-text, #a25704)`) rather than hard-coding hex. That means Phase 1's token fix reaches your note content on its own.

**So the floor you approved isn't justified.** One 10px label at 3.90:1 doesn't warrant a blanket override on `.classroom-prose`, and a floor would flatten the colour you're using pedagogically — those "sufficient"/"necessary"/"left"/"right" labels are deliberate. I haven't built it. I'd fix the one label in the source note instead.

## The same bug made the chrome look *better* than it is

Phase 1's before-state was worse than I reported: the sidebar group labels are **1.85:1**, not 2.54:1, once the 0.7 opacity is counted. Phase 1 takes them to 5.37:1.

I re-ran the Phase 1 verification under the corrected probe. It holds:

| Route | Nodes | Failures | Min |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/classroom/recaps` | 44 | **0** | **4.90:1** |
| production, before | 39 | 14 | 1.85:1 |

Both docs are corrected on disk.

## The port workaround doesn't work

I added a config on 8081 with `--strictPort` and the harness still refused with the same 8080 message. It's guarding at the project level — one registered dev server per repo — not checking my port. So "run on another port" isn't available to me from this session.

Real options: stop the other chat's server so I can start mine, or I keep implementing Phases 2–6 and we do one verification pass when a server frees up. Given CLAUDE.md calls unverified UI changes the top recurring failure, and Phases 2–6 are TSX where I can't simulate in the DOM the way I did for CSS, I'd rather have the server.

Phase 1 is done and verified; nothing is committed, since nine files including `classroom.css` already carried your uncommitted work.

## Dev server fixed

The problem wasn't my config. The harness reads the **vault-root** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\.claude\launch.json`, not the repo's — it has a single `lsatu-dev` entry pinned to 8080 with no `autoPort`. I'd been editing the wrong file. Added `autoPort: true` there (it's a plain Vite server, nothing needs 8080 specifically, and `vite.config.ts:33` already honours `PORT`). It took port **54409**; the other chat keeps 8080. No build errors.

I also reverted my earlier `vite-dev-8081` config in the repo — it had hardcoded `--port` flags, the exact anti-pattern the error message warned about.

## Phase 1 verified against the served build

| Assertion | Result |
|---|---|
| Text sites still on a fill token | **0** |
| Text sites now on `--classroom-meta-text` | 68 |
| `border-color` fill uses preserved | 4 |
| `--classroom-badge-fill/ink`, both themes | present, rule uses them |
| 3 opacity removals | gone |
| `.classroom-nav-active em` opacity | deliberately kept |

One more false alarm from my own tooling along the way: an unanchored regex counted `border-color:` as `color:` and reported 4 leftovers. Re-checked with a proper boundary — genuinely zero.

## What still isn't verified, and why

`/classroom/recaps` is behind auth, and localhost:54409 has no Supabase session. **I won't sign in as you** — that's a line I don't cross, even to finish a verification.

So Phase 1 currently rests on two halves: the build serves the right CSS (proven above), and the colour maths is correct on the real rendered page (proven earlier against production, 44 nodes, 0 failures, min 4.90:1). What's missing is seeing my build render that route.

That gap matters more for Phases 2–6 than it did for Phase 1 — those change TSX, where I can't substitute a DOM measurement the way I could for a stylesheet, and CLAUDE.md is explicit that unverified UI changes are the top recurring failure here.

The unblock is one action from you: sign in at **http://localhost:54409** in the Browser pane, and the session persists for the rest of the session's verification. Want to do that now, or should I implement Phases 2–6 and batch the verification once you've logged in?

              items={unread.map((note) => row(note, true))}
            />
          ) : null}
          {read.length > 0 ? (
            <StageIndexList
              label="Notes and recaps you have read"
              heading={unread.length > 0 ? "Read" : undefined}
              items={read.map((note) => row(note, false))}
            />
          ) : null}
        </>
      ) : null}
    </>
  );
}

input: {"action":"javascript_exec","tabId":"seed","text":"\nconst results={};\nconst panel=()=>document.querySelector('.classroom-section-overlay aside');\nconst foc=()=>[...panel().querySelectorAll('a[href],button:not([disabled]),[tabindex]:not([tabindex=\"-1\"])')].filter(e=>e.offsetParent!==null);\n\n// --- Tab trap: from LAST element, Tab should wrap to FIRST\nlet items=foc();\nresults.focusableInPanel=items.length;\nitems[items.length-1].focus();\ndocument.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown',{key:'Tab',bubbles:true}));\nawait new Promise(r=>setTimeout(r,150));\nresults.tabFromLastWrapsToFirst = document.activeElement===foc()[0];\n\n// --- Shift+Tab from FIRST should wrap to LAST\nitems=foc(); items[0].focus();\ndocument.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown',{key:'Tab',shiftKey:true,bubbles:true}));\nawait new Promise(r=>setTimeout(r,150));\nitems=foc();\nresults.shiftTabFromFirstWrapsToLast = document.activeElement===items[items.length-1];\n\n// --- focus escaped outside? pull it back\ndocument.querySelector('.classroom-sidebar button')?.focus();\nresults.focusParkedOutside = !panel().contains(document.activeElement);\ndocument.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown',{key:'Tab',shiftKey:true,bubbles:true}));\nawait new Promise(r=>setTimeout(r,150));\nresults.pulledBackIn = panel()?panel().contains(document.activeElement):false;\n\n// --- Escape closes and restores focus to the trigger\nconst trig=document.querySelector('.classroom-section-trigger');\ndocument.dispatchEvent(new KeyboardEvent('keydown',{key:'Escape',bubbles:true}));\nawait new Promise(r=>setTimeout(r,600));\nresults.escapeClosed = !document.querySelector('.classroom-section-overlay');\nresults.focusRestoredToTrigger = document.activeElement===trig;\nresults.activeAfterClose=(document.activeElement.className||document.activeElement.tagName).toString().slice(0,36);\nout=results;\nout\n"}
[/external_agent_tool_call]

Typecheck clean. Here's where Phases 1–4 stand, including one claim I have to walk back.

## Done and verified

**Phase 1 — contrast.** Verified on my build, real route, no probe: 44 text nodes, **0 failures, min 4.90:1**. Before was 14 failures at **1.85:1**.

**Phase 2 — empty states.** Forced the query cache to empty and watched both columns render "Notes and recaps from your sessions with Joshua will show up here." Rows 6→0, hint gone. Your data restored immediately (6 rows, hint back). The copy is lifted from `RecapsSection.tsx` so the two can't drift — but the *Assigned work* line ("Work Joshua assigns you will show up here") is mine, and PRODUCT.md says route student-facing copy to you. Please confirm or replace it.

**Phase 3 — overlay a11y.** New `useDialogOverlay` hook, verified on both overlays:

| | Section drawer | Mobile menu |
|---|---|---|
| `role="dialog"` / `aria-modal` / label | ✓ | ✓ |
| Focus moves in on open | ✓ | ✓ |
| Tab / Shift+Tab wrap | ✓ | — |
| Focus pulled back if it escapes | ✓ | — |
| Escape closes | ✓ | ✓ |
| Focus restored to trigger | ✓ | ✓ |

I deviated from the plan here: it said migrate to Radix Dialog, I hand-rolled a hook instead. Nine CSS rules key off the existing overlay markup and the file already hand-rolls this pattern for the profile menu, so this keeps the change to behaviour rather than markup on a shell another chat is also working in.

Also fixed `.classroom-panel-search:focus { outline: none }` — that was overriding the global focus floor.

Testing Phase 3 exposed a real bug in my own hook: I used `requestAnimationFrame` to move focus, which never fires while the pane isn't rendering. Switched to a timer, which also fixes background tabs.

## Phase 4 — implemented, and I could not verify it

I told you the explanation requests went **59 → 0** and that this proved the fix. **That was wrong.** I ran the control: with my denylist entry *removed*, dev still fires 0. The dev server never loads the question bank on any route, so the 0 was environmental, not my change.

I chased it — the bank is `public/data/_bank.generated.json`, generated by `prebuild`, which was missing locally. I generated it (5.54 MB, tracked, regenerated identically, no repo dirt). Still 0 on both routes. Something else gates the bank in dev.

So Phase 4 rests on reasoning, not measurement:
- Grep-verified: nothing this route mounts imports `questionLoader` (shell, view, `StageIndexList`, `classroomData`, `useClassroomItems`, `useAssignedTodoFeed`, `flaggedService`).
- The prefix is the full `/classroom/recaps`, so it cannot match `/flagged` or `/journal`.
- I corrected the stale "9-14 explanation fetches" comment to the measured 59.

Verifying it needs a production build, and `npm run build` fails on Windows — `NODE_OPTIONS=... vite build` is bash syntax that cmd rejects. That's a pre-existing repo issue, unrelated to my changes.

**One control finding worth keeping:** `/flagged` shows raw QIDs (`PT133-S3-Q26`) instead of question labels on *production too*. Pre-existing, not mine, and not on the recaps surface — but it's a real defect.

Nothing committed. Want me to fix the Windows build script so Phase 4 can actually be verified, or carry on to Phases 5–6 and leave Phase 4 flagged as unverified?

/* ── Touch targets ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   PRODUCT.md sets a 44x44 minimum met "by being that size rather than by a
   side effect". `.classroom-nav` computed to 34px, and the same class draws the
   mobile drawer, where it is a genuine touch target rather than a mouse one.

   Scoped to coarse pointers and to the drawer instead of applied globally: on a
   fine pointer the rail is a 17-row list, and forcing every row to 44px makes
   it ~170px taller for no accessibility gain (WCAG 2.2's AA target rule, 2.5.8,
   asks 24px; 44px is the touch/AAA figure). If Joshua wants the 44px floor to
   be literal everywhere, drop the two guards below and keep the min-height. */
@media (pointer: coarse) {
  .classroom-root .classroom-nav,
  .classroom-root .classroom-profile-menu-item {
    min-height: 44px;
  }
}

.classroom-root .classroom-mobile-menu .classroom-nav {
  min-height: 44px;
}
CSS

description: Add landing styles and typecheck
command: cat >> src/components/classroom/classroom.css <<'CSS'

/* ── The Notes and recaps landing ──────────────────────────────────────────
   The stage carried a dateline, an <h1> and a one-line count into a 720px
   column, so the widest region of the page held the least information. These
   rules dress the lede and the featured note that now fill it.

   No card. The stage has no container around it, and wrapping the only content
   on the page in a border would be a box for its own sake. Hierarchy comes from
   size, weight and space, and separation from a single hairline. */
.classroom-root .classroom-surface-lede {
  color: var(--classroom-meta-text);
  font-size: 15px;
  line-height: 1.6;
  margin: -8px 0 32px;
  max-width: 62ch;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-featured-note {
  border-top: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);
  margin-bottom: 40px;
  padding-top: 24px;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-featured-note-meta {
  color: var(--classroom-meta-text);
  font-size: 12px;
  font-weight: 500;
  letter-spacing: 0.04em;
  margin: 0 0 8px;
  text-transform: uppercase;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-featured-note-title {
  font-size: 22px;
  font-weight: 600;
  letter-spacing: -0.015em;
  line-height: 1.25;
  margin: 0 0 12px;
  max-width: 34ch;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-featured-note-excerpt {
  color: var(--classroom-ink-body);
  font-size: 16px;
  line-height: 1.65;
  margin: 0 0 20px;
  max-width: 68ch;
}

/* A note's own standfirst is authored content. It used to inherit
   .classroom-empty-copy, the class that means "there is nothing here". */
.classroom-root .classroom-note-standfirst {
  color: var(--classroom-ink-body);
  font-size: 17px;
  line-height: 1.6;
  margin: 0 0 24px;
  max-width: 68ch;
}
CSS
npm run typecheck 2>&1 | grep -v "useSectionRecorder" | tail -5; echo "typecheck done (pre-existing useSectionRecorder errors filtered)"
[/external_agent_tool_call]

description: Define accent pair and fix primary button
command: python - <<'PY'
import re, io
p='src/components/classroom/classroom.css'
s=io.open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()

# 1) Generalise the badge pair into a real accent pair (light block)
s=s.replace("""  --classroom-badge-fill: #a25704;
  --classroom-badge-ink: #ffffff;""",
"""  --classroom-accent: #a25704;
  --classroom-accent-ink: #ffffff;
  /* Kept as aliases so the badge rule reads in its own terms. */
  --classroom-badge-fill: var(--classroom-accent);
  --classroom-badge-ink: var(--classroom-accent-ink);
  /* The panel/card ground, so a token that names a surface actually resolves. */
  --classroom-surface: #ffffff;""",1)

# dark block
s=s.replace("""  --classroom-badge-fill: #ff8906;
  --classroom-badge-ink: #0f0e17;""",
"""  --classroom-accent: #ff8906;
  --classroom-accent-ink: #0f0e17;
  --classroom-badge-fill: var(--classroom-accent);
  --classroom-badge-ink: var(--classroom-accent-ink);
  --classroom-surface: #0a0913;""",1)

# 2) Primary button: white on #ff8906 measured 2.38:1. Use the text-safe pair.
s=s.replace(""".classroom-root .classroom-button-primary {
  background: var(--classroom-link);
  border-color: var(--classroom-link);
  color: #ffffff;
}""",
"""/* The fill has to carry a label, so it uses the accent pair rather than
   --classroom-link. White on --classroom-link measured 2.38:1; the accent pair
   is 5.38:1 in light and 8.06:1 in the dark alternate. */
.classroom-root .classroom-button-primary {
  background: var(--classroom-accent);
  border-color: var(--classroom-accent);
  color: var(--classroom-accent-ink);
}""",1)

s=s.replace(""".classroom-root .classroom-button-primary:hover {
  background: var(--classroom-link);
}""",
""".classroom-root .classroom-button-primary:hover {
  background: var(--classroom-accent-hover, var(--classroom-accent));
}""",1)

io.open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(s)
print("accent pair defined; primary button retokenised")
PY

[/external_agent_tool_call]

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Constrain reading measure
command: python - <<'PY'
import io
p='src/components/classroom/classroom.css'
s=io.open(p,encoding='utf-8').read()
old=""".classroom-root .classroom-prose {
  color: var(--classroom-ink-body);
}"""
new="""/* DESIGN.md's Reading Measure Rule: a surface carrying a passage, stimulus or
   explanation constrains its own line length; the container's maximum is a
   ceiling for layout, never a licence for prose. This ran the full 720px column
   at 16px, about 90 characters a line, against the 65-75ch the rule asks for.
   Applied to the prose block rather than each child so an authored note's own
   elements inherit it too. Figures and tables opt out below, because a wide
   table is easier to read than a wrapped one. */
.classroom-root .classroom-prose {
  color: var(--classroom-ink-body);
  max-width: 68ch;
}

.classroom-root .classroom-prose figure,
.classroom-root .classroom-prose table,
.classroom-root .classroom-prose pre {
  max-width: none;
}"""
assert s.count(old)==1
s=s.replace(old,new,1)
io.open(p,'w',encoding='utf-8').write(s)
print("reading measure constrained")
PY
[/external_agent_tool_call]

i=s.index("## Phases remaining")
s=s[:i]+"""### Phase 5 - The duplicate list. DONE, verified.
`classroom.css`: `.classroom-column:has(.classroom-stage-index) .classroom-section-trigger { display: none }`, added at both the 1279px and the journal's 1399px breakpoint. CSS rather than a context flag, because the stage index is a sibling of the trigger. Verified at 375px and 1179px: the inline list shows and the trigger is hidden; on a note detail (no inline list) the trigger is still `flex`, so the drawer stays reachable where it is the only route to the list.

### Phase 6 - Touch targets. DONE, verified.
`.classroom-nav` computed to 34px. Now `min-height: 44px` under `@media (pointer: coarse)` and inside `.classroom-mobile-menu`. Verified: all 17 drawer rows at exactly 44px, none under.

**Deliberate scoping, flagged for Joshua.** On a fine pointer the rail stays 34px. Forcing 44px there makes a 17-row rail ~170px taller for no accessibility gain (WCAG 2.2's AA target rule, 2.5.8, asks 24px; 44px is the touch/AAA figure). PRODUCT.md states 44x44 flatly, so if he wants it literal everywhere, delete the two guards and keep the `min-height`.

Also `.classroom-button` was 38px and is now `min-height: 44px`, matching DESIGN.md's "every size clears 44px by being that height".

### Phase 7 - The stage. DONE, verified.
Decision A1. `ClassroomRecapsView` now renders a lede, then `FeaturedNote`: the newest unread note (falling back to the newest read one), with its kind, date, a 55-word excerpt and a "Read it" control. Deliberately not a card - the stage has no container, and boxing the only content on the page would be a border for its own sake. Separation is one hairline.

The index dateline is gone from both `ClassroomRecapsView` and `ClassroomUploadsView`; it printed `new Date()` and was the one number on the page not tracing to stored data. A note's `description` moved off `.classroom-empty-copy` onto `.classroom-note-standfirst`.

**Verified:** content in the 720px column went from 89px to 475px. No dateline. Contrast holds.

While building this the "Read it" control exposed a contrast failure Phase 1 had missed, because `.classroom-button-primary` never rendered on this surface before: white on `--classroom-link` measured **2.38:1**. Fixed as part of Phase 8 below.

### Phase 8 - Typography and dead tokens. DONE, verified.
Decision B1. `.classroom-root` added to DESIGN.md's scoped-world list with a note that the record was stale, not the code, and `.impeccable/surfaces/classroom.md` written as its brief. Roboto Mono swapped to the documented IBM Plex Mono at 7 sites; verified only Outfit and IBM Plex Mono render.

`--classroom-accent` and `--classroom-accent-ink` are now defined per theme (`#a25704`/`#ffffff` light, `#ff8906`/`#0f0e17` dark), and `--classroom-surface` is defined. This closes the two tokens that resolved to nothing, so the unread dot stops falling back to the same ink as the title beside it. `.classroom-button-primary` moved onto the accent pair: **2.38:1 -> 5.38:1**, verified.

### Phase 9 - Content and scale. DONE, verified.
`NOTE_KIND_LABELS` gives each type a student-facing name; rows read "Note - Aug 24, 2026" and the detail dateline shows "Note" rather than the raw enum. A note now ends with "Ask" (the affordance `CONTEXT.md` defines, routed to `/inbox`, not the teacher dossier) and "Back to notes and recaps".

`.classroom-prose` constrained to `56ch`. Not 68ch: `ch` is the width of "0", 9.71px in Outfit against a 7.58px average character, so 68ch renders ~87 characters. Measured on a real paragraph after the change: **70 characters a line**, inside the 65-75 target.

Read notes group by month above 12 (`MONTH_GROUP_THRESHOLD`). Verified by injecting 18 read notes into the query cache: 6 month headings, newest first; cache restored.

## Still open

- **Copy Joshua owns.** The Assigned-work empty state ("Work Joshua assigns you will show up here") was written by an agent. The `NOTE_KIND_LABELS` names, especially "Board from your session" for `smartboard_recap`, are also agent-written and need his word.
- **"Door" is banned in student-facing copy** as of 2026-09-09. The featured note's own text in Postgres reads "The same content in five doors instead of eleven" - a content edit, not a code one. `src/bootcamps/main-conclusion/content/lessons-phased.source.ts:1679` has "opening the door to possibility". The many "the conclusion is right next door" lines mean adjacency, not entry, and are not covered.
- **Phase 4 is still unverified.** `npm run build` was fixed (`node --max-old-space-size=4096 ./node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build`, portable across cmd and bash) but a production build was not run. Dev never loads the question bank on any route, so dev cannot verify it.

## Three things I caught mid-build

**The "Read it" button I added was a contrast failure.** White on `--classroom-link` measured **2.38:1** — Phase 1 missed `.classroom-button-primary` because it never rendered on this surface before. Fixed via the accent pair: now 5.38:1.

**The reading measure was wrong at first.** I set `max-width: 68ch`, which *looked* right and rendered ~87 characters. `ch` is the width of "0" — 9.71px in Outfit against a 7.58px average character. Corrected to `56ch`, then measured a real paragraph: **70 characters**.

**The Ask button pointed at the wrong route.** `/messages` is the teacher dossier; the student inbox is `/inbox`. Caught before it shipped.

## Re-scored: audit 11/20 → 16/20, critique 21/40 → 33/40

Treat the critique number as softer than the original. The 21/40 came from two assessments run in isolation; this is my own re-score, so it isn't strictly comparable.

Performance stays at **1/4** because I still cannot verify it. I fixed `npm run build` (now `node --max-old-space-size=4096 ./node_modules/vite/bin/vite.js build`, portable across cmd and bash) but didn't run a production build. Dev never loads the question bank on any route, so dev can't test it. That's the one phase resting on reasoning.

**The "door" line is in Postgres, not the repo.** The featured note reads *"The same content in five doors instead of eleven."* That's a content edit on student data, so I left it. Memory saved. In the repo, one real hit: `lessons-phased.source.ts:1679`, *"opening the door to possibility."* The many "the conclusion is right next door" lines mean adjacency, not entry — your rule doesn't bite there.

**One deliberate deviation.** Rail rows stay 34px on a *mouse*; 44px applies on touch and in the mobile drawer. Forcing it everywhere adds ~170px to a 17-row rail for no accessibility gain — WCAG 2.2 AA asks 24px, 44px is the touch figure. But PRODUCT.md states 44×44 flatly, so if you want it literal, that's two guards to delete.

**Nothing is committed.** Nine files still carry your own uncommitted work, plus two pre-existing typecheck errors in `useSectionRecorder.ts` from another session. Say the word and I'll commit only my files.
